Sat Aug  8 09:08:20 2026 Well,
that. But the main event is
spiking interhub. Could we say:

  In fourteen days we will be
  able to see a FB taking over
  one entire Blackhole card's
  worth of the matrix

as an interim countdown? We have
a bunch of non-mfmx stuff we
have to deal with during that
time, as well, but let's MAKE
THAT CLAIM and see if we can hit
it.

So I've been thinking: You Know,
Dave.. Couldn't we do something
that would pick a subset of
cells and declare them 'in
charge' for a period of time,
and while they are In Charge
they can run events in their own
space PLUS some distance into
their neighbors space.

The idea is slow-T6-locking
rather than fast EW locking,
with the intention of accepting
some limited spatiotemporal
anisotropies to amortize
regional cache communication
costs.

And what we should do now is
explore that possibility a bit
more concretely via this list of
things TODO:

Sun Aug  9 08:03:55 2026 So now
I'm thinking:

 - What if we were completely
   disgusting and just had the
   host to control the
   leadership?

 - We'd reserve a small 'host
   control' L1 region L1 in all
   T6s (not just hubs), and

 - Host would multicast commands
   to that region, which would
   be ignored by images that
   didn't need or know or match
   the host control command du
   jour. One tiny fucking
   multicast (per BH) to reach
   all the (in this case) hubs.

 - Our first host control
   command thing would be, say,
   "interhub phasing". Interhub
   Phasing would define, say:

 - A target+cmd byte (say),
   maybe 4b of who this is for
   (HUB) and 4b of cmd,

 - A spinner byte (say) so
   target can reliably recognize
   when the host control command
   changes, and

 - Two bytes (say) of per-cmd
   data.

[34:

Sun Aug  9 08:16:18 2026 And for
the HUB/INTERHUB host control,
the key argument would be the
SUPERCELL PHASE, a number from
0-9, say, indicating:

 9 : No new interhub activity

 0 : The cell at supercell
     coords (0,0) is major

 1 : The cell at supercell
     coords (1,0) is major

2-8 : The cell at supercell
      coords (2,0)..(2,2) is
      major

[35:

Sun Aug  9 08:38:48 2026 Not
sure if 'major' is what we want
for "the hub that can currently
write its Moore neighborhood of
other hubs", but that's the idea
we're going for, anyway. (Were
calling it 'leader' I think,
before.)

Now, I'm not sure if we might
want to try to keep all ngbs
'cache bumper' regions up to
date all the time, or if we want
to have subphases within a major
phase, like:
 - refresh major cache
 - major runs superregion EWs +
   minors run subregions EWs
 - drain: nobody initiates any
   superregion events
[36:

Sun Aug  9 08:56:28 2026 Or it
might be a bit more complicated
than that, maybe more like:

(0) Drain: Nobody initiates new
    superregion EWs, and
    'all' old ones finish
(1) New Major: Host broadcasts
    supercell coords of the new
    major (if any, maybe not?)
(2) Cache refresh: ngbs send
    their relevant sites to the
    new major, or to a hub
    closer to the new major, for
    forwarding to the new major
    (to avoid a round the world
    trip).
    They copy the spinner seq#
    to various places to signal
    forwarding completion.

[39:

Sun Aug  9 14:52:02 2026 I guess
I'm going to hang my hat on "16B
alignment" for L1<->L1 packets,
which means arbitrary
subrectangles of arrays really
aren't going to be acceptable,
even in strips.

So if we are facing copying
before and/or after
transmission, that pushes me
back toward ACB-like packets.

Could we make a somewhat more
general packet router segment,
that would 'automatically'
forward bank shot packets?

The problem is always
initiative: When can the sender
be sure the next packet can be
sent? That's why we keep
ping-ponging packets between
destinations: The next packet
can be sent once the/an
appropriate 'car' has returned
to you, not before.

[56:

Tue Aug 11 10:32:12 2026 So,
increasingly here, I'm thinking
if I really want to commit to
the bit (just go fast on QB/BH),
I should embrace not GALS -
"Globally Asynchronous, Locally
Synchronous" - but LAGS -
"Locally Asynchronous, Globally
Synchronous".

With the idea being that the
host can multicast 'phase
information' to the fleet and
that's cheap. And the tiles can,
each in their own time, react to
the phase update and then push
some confirmation back to the
host. And the host can block
until all confirmations are back
or the cows come home or
whatever the phase-end criteria
ends up being in any given case.

And the phases could be like

1: SuperCell Hub (X,Y) Is Chosen
   At Random By The Host

2: Non-chosen hubs drain EWPs,
   then send caches to Chosen,
   and switch to doing hidden
   EWPs

3: Chosen hub receives all
   caches and switches to Fish
   In The Middle EWPs

4: Host Says Nobody Fish In The
   Middle: Chosen hub stops
   doing in the middle EWPs and
   acks when all have drained.

5: Chosen hub pushes all caches
   back to non-chosen hubs.

6: Loop

[57:

Tue Aug 11 11:13:28 2026 Aand,
we note, this is not a new
Indefinite-Scalability-Crime,
because after all, mfms has done
the same thing for years -
starting and stopping events
globally, for rendering and
statistics.

[58:

Tue Aug 11 11:15:29 2026 So I'm
thinking a single word to
scatter out, and a single word
(per T6) to (push)gather back,
maybe with a structure like:

struct PhaseInfo {
  u8 mSpinner;
  u8 mPhase;
  u8 mArg;
  u8 mReserved; // or response maybe
};

And every T6 tile would have
that stored in the SAME
well-known place low in L1. We
don't want to put it in the
HostBlock because the HostBlock
position - at the end of the
image - depends on the image
size. [59:

Tue Aug 11 11:20:20 2026
Thinking Phase/command 0 should
always be PAUSE, which causes
the T6 to ack the command and
then sit on a memory fence until
a non-0-command appears.[60:

Tue Aug 18 16:19:16 2026 Well
that's not really a to-do list
but we'll see.

So I'm thinking:

1. Host decides to send
   something. Let's say for now
   that decision is at the
   QuietBox level.

2. Host builds a PhaserBlock in
   a QuietBox level location.
   With controlled incremented
   nonce and whatever
   particulars are needed.

3. Host dishes that to each BH
   (in a random order might be
   nice) who multicasts it to
   its fleet at the well-known
   T6_PHASER_ADDR address.

4. T6/hn sees a complete
   PhaserBlock packet at the
   T6_PHASER_ADDR address, with
   a nonce different than its
   last-known nonce.

[98: Tue Aug 18 21:18:58 2026

5. hn takes first crack at the
   PhaserBlock. Suppose it can
   handle it completely on its
   own.

6. It does so, then marks the
   PhaserBlock 'handled' in some
   way, then sends the whole
   thing back to the host.

7. Once a PhaserBlock has been
   marked 'handled', it is
   ignored by everybody T6 side.
   So it's okay to leave a
   handled PhaserBlock at the
   T6_PHASER_ADDR address.

8. Host iterates over the
   returns, counting how many
   T6s have replied with the
   current nonce.

9. Host waits until the count is
   full and then the phaserbolt
   is complete.

Sun Aug 23 21:58:52 2026 Well,
what are our possible task
action decisions?

   worker if we every have any)

 - HOLD: do not route but remind
   me about this again later

 - DONE: this task is complete;
   delete it and reclaim
   resources

That might be enough to get
started.. And each possible task
worker ought to be able ask the
TM what's their current task, if
any.

And I guess we'd want to have at
least ROUTE be able to fail (or
turn into HOLD temporarily) if
we get buffers full somewhere..

    before responding?

Sat Aug 29 11:01:58 2026 Well
let's take a crack at using the
whole grid. The scheme would be:

(0) Suspend event processing

(1) Wait a "long time" for
    events to drain, because we
    haven't implemented the
    delay-til-drained phaser
    reply that we wanted to.

(2) Choose at random in
    (0..1,0..1) to pick the
    leader of each 2x2 supercell

(3) Shoot that out to the fleet.
    Each supercell hub
    determines whether it is
    leader, which tells it
    whether it will shrink or
    expand its event region.

(4) Using the existing contents
    of the full grid (which are
    supposedly now up-to-date
    globally), write to the
    caches of each supercell
    leader. We'd like it if we
    could do that in parallel
    across the BHs, which means
    we want to tell a per-BH
    thread to do the writing, I
    guess.

(5) After the writing is done to
    all supercell leader caches,
    resume event processing.
    Leaders place event centers
    no less than 4 (EW radius)
    away from their cache
    boundaries; followers place
    event centers at least 4 (EW
    radius) + 10 (cache width)
    away from their local grid
    boundaries.

(6) Wait a loooong time for some
    stuff to happen.

(7) Suspend event processing and
    wait AGAIN.

(8) Using the existing contents
    of the full grid (which are
    now again supposedly up to
    date, including the cache
    regions the supercell
    leaders were doing events
    on), write the relevant
    caches back to all the
    follower hubs.

(9) Go to (2) until the cows
    come home.

[320:

Fri Sep 11 16:18:43 2026 Oy
that's a lot of stuff. How can
we simplify our thinking about
it?

The 'main purposes' I can
remember are:

 - For each type of block:

    #define BLOCKCODE_LIST()                                \
      XX(CELLBLOCK,CLBK,sizeof(CellBlock),CellBlock,1u)     \
      XX(PHASER,PHSR,sizeof(PhaserBlock),PhaserBlock,1u)    \
      XX(LOGBLOCK,LGBK,4096,LogBlockStg,1u)                 \
      XX(ZOTBLOCK,ZTBK,256,ZotBlockStg,2u)                  \
      XX(EWHUB,EWHB,2048,EwpBlockStg,8u)                    \
      XX(EWPCARS,EWCR,2048,EwpBlockStg,1u)                  \
      XX(INTERHUB,IHUB,20800,InterHubStorage,4u)            \
      XX(T6GRID,T6G,242692,T6Grid,1u)                       \
      XX(ACACHEBLOCK,ACB,5120,ACacheBlockStg,1u)            \
      //END OF BLOCK_CODE_LIST

there is potentially storage for
one or more of those blocks, in
T6 L1 and/or host RAM, depending
on the block type and the T6
image we're considering.

Hart NC pretty much uniformly
handles the arrivals and
departures of cars on the T6s -
running subclassed recvTC() and
shipTC() methods as appropriate.

Hart NC initializes (in ways we
are NOT going to talk about now)
and eventually calls

  liveNC(hb) ->
  justLive(hb) ->
   loop forever -> stepNC(hb)

and stepNC(hb) - running the
same code on all images - does
basically this:

      int stepNC(HostBlock & hb) {
        TaskWorker::updateHartTasks();
        RCFlag res = fNC.runHTFuncsNC(HTOpCode::HTOC_LIVE);
        return 0;
      }

In turn:

(1) updateHartTasks() runs
    different code on different
    images:

./images/ewp/src/EwpTaskManager.cpp 26:  void TaskWorker::updateHartTasks() {
./images/hub/src/HubTaskManager.cpp 70:  void TaskWorker::updateHartTasks() {

while

(2) runHTFuncsNC() iterates
    through an array of
    functions - mostly defined
    on a per-image basis:

    ./images/ewp/src/EwpBlock.cpp 98:  __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))
    ./images/hub/src/EP_ACacheBlock.cpp 567:  __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))
    ./images/hub/src/EwpBlock.cpp 77:  __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))
    ./images/hub/src/InterHub.cpp 124:  __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))
    ./images/zot/src/ZotBlock.cpp 80:  __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))

    but one key one common to
    all images:

      __attribute__((section(".rodata_fp_table_nc")))
      HTFuncPtr logEPPtr = &manageLogBlockNC;

which, as example, goes
something like this:

      static RCFlag manageLogBlockNC(HTOpCode htoc)
 -> myLogBlockEPNC.updateOps();
 -> ./shared/include/EP.tcc 32:  bool EP<SUBEP,SUBTC>::updateOps() {
 ->  RECEIVE CARS:
   .. if (!car.isComplete()) break;
   .. if (!isArriving(cs)) break;
   .. if (recvTC(car, mOldestGone))
     SHIP CARS
   .. SUBTC * carp = getClosedTCPtrIfAny();
      if (!carp) break; // nothing ready to go
      SUBTC & car = *carp;
   .. if (!isDeparting(cs)) break; // (still) not ready to go
   .. if (shipTC(car,mOldestHere))

where recvTC and shipTC are
subclassed as mentioned earlier,
but with an important
difference: shipTC is ONLY
subclassed by T6EP

Sun Sep 13 01:09:44 2026 Well
tracing that all the way through
was actually a little bit
satisfying.. possibly a little
bit helpful.

In particular there are fewer
locks to be thinking about, in
the specifically InterHub code
paths, than I was imagining.

Really, just one lock per
InterHubStorage block, of which
we have four, to provide
elevator platforms for our
up-to-four regional hub
neighbors.

And we'd need to use those locks
when? Whenever there might be a
race between HN, looking to
route an IH car, and HB, looking
to originate or retire one. Yes?

[329:

Sun Sep 13 01:29:01 2026 So it's
seeming to me now that that's
one of the keys to getting to
implementability here.

And another one is the invariant
that HN is always supposed to be
able to bail on processing an
incoming packet, and deal with
it again later (but also still
next - no within-block car
reordering, we're trying to
say).

I don't know that that second
one is actually true of the code
at the moment, but that's what
we're going to design/debug
towards anyway, I think.

No within-block car reordering
permitted, but since we're
saying we can DEFER dealing with
a car in a block - and go on to
other blocks - that amounts to
saying that between-block
reordering IS allowed.

And that between-block
reordering is supposed to allow
everything to be well-founded
and drain out eventually. Or
else we designed the
higher-level stuff wrong.

Sun Sep 13 08:58:13 2026
So-oh-oh, that all seems kind of
plausible. It seems we could
expand IH::recvTC something
like:

  bool InterHubEP::recvTC(InterHubBlock & car, u8 carindex) {

    // CHECK FOR ROUTING
    if (car.needsRouting())
      return tryRouting(car,carindex);

    // OTHERWISE HANDLE LOCALLY
    Super::L1Data::CarIdxRB & crbi = getCarIdxs().mTheIdxs[Super::L1Data::CarIdxs::COMM2COMP];
    if (crbi.isFull()) return false; // bail if can't notify??

    // Open it up
    car.openTC();
    crbi.add(carindex); //notify hB

    return true;
  }

with something like:

  bool InterHubEP::needsRouting(InterHubBlock & car) {
    // PLATFORM LOCK HELD
    ..stuf..

where ..stuf.. would mostly
check for non-(0,0) destination,
I guess. And like:

  bool InterHubEP::tryRouting(InterHubBlock & car, u8 carindex) {
    // PLATFORM LOCK HELD

which would do the hard part -

(1) figuring out which way to go
    in some deterministic way
    (for now at least),

(2) acquiring the lock on that
    outbound platform, so we are
    now holding two EP_InterHub
    locks,

(3) checking if there are any
    available cars, and
    returning false if not
    (releasing the second lock,
    and note we're not really
    sure what 'available' means
    in this context),

(4) drafting the first such car,
    copying our car arg to that,
    updating its routing info
    and its status and its
    platform status so that it's
    shippable like an IH car
    that was originated here,

(5) doing the same with our car
    argument, so it looks like
    any other that has just been
    received successfully,

(6) and finally returning true
    to recvTC() - releasing the
    lock on the outbound
    platform -

so updateOps will adjust state
so that car is now available for
local loading and shipping..

And That's All There Is To It.

[334:

Sun Sep 13 09:59:46 2026 So, on
the question of 'checking for
available cars', if we keep the
existing flow in place as much
as possible, that causes a
problem because the normal
non-routed IH car flow gives
control of 'available' cars to
HB, via placing the carindex in
the appropriate COMM2COMP crb -
and then HB expects to pop from
that crb without taking a lock,
because HB owns that end of the
crb.

Which is all why, by the way, we
originally thought HB should be
involved in routing. Hrghmmm.
[335:

Sun Sep 13 11:18:50 2026 This
sounds pretty scary but what if
we tried to reserve one IH car
for HN's routing use, and only
put the second and subsequent
cars into the COMM2COMP crb?
Then we'd be able to forward a
single IH car without HB
involvement, and we'd snarf up
the next free car to replenish
our forwarding slot.

That would require HN to be able
to detect when arriving cars are
'empty', but I'm not sure that's
semantics that are currently
presented at the HN level?
Another reason to be scared of
the idea.

Sun Sep 13 15:50:24 2026 And in

    bool update(bool inside) {
      MFM_API_ASSERT(isValid(),ILLEGAL_STATE);
      if (!isValid()) return false;

      ///// FIRST DO ROUTING IF NEEDED, FOR ALL PAYLOAD TYPES
      IHPHeader & h = mIHPHeader;
      if (h.mHops2Dest.length() > 0) {
        // which way do we go george?

        // maybe for starters don't be random? so
        // we can have hope that packet sequences
        // will arrive in order?

        U8C steps = h.mHops2Dest.abs();
        if (steps.x >= steps.y) { // go EW
          if (h.mHops2Dest.x > 0) return tryRoute(D4_E,inside);
          if (h.mHops2Dest.x < 0) return tryRoute(D4_W,inside);
          FAIL(UNREACHABLE_CODE); // hey, dest.length() > 0...
        } else /* steps.y > steps.x */ {     // go NS
          if (h.mHops2Dest.y > 0) return tryRoute(D4_S,inside);
          if (h.mHops2Dest.y < 0) return tryRoute(D4_N,inside);
          FAIL(UNREACHABLE_CODE); // hey, dest.length() > 0...
        }
      }

      ///// WE'RE STILL HERE AFTER ROUTING
      switch (h.mPayloadType) {
      case IHPT_EMPTY: return false;
      case IHPT_PING:
        return ((IHPPing*) &mIHPData[0])->update(inside);
      case IHPT_ATOMS:
        return ((IHPAtoms*) &mIHPData[0])->update(inside);
      default:
        FAIL(ILLEGAL_STATE);
      }
    }

   ACacheBlockPrivateControl,
   which we have not C&Pd but
   there's also a
   ACacheBlockPrivateControl::ACacheBlockL1Control
   * mACBL1Control; that gets
   initted, apparently.

 - An instance of
   ACacheBlockPrivateControl -
   myPACBControlH0 - gets
   declared in H0 fast local.

 - And
   int ACacheBlockPrivateControl::updateCars(HostBlock & hb)
   extracts the info:

        ACacheBlockL1Control & acl1 = *mACBL1Control;
        ACacheBlock * curacb = acl1.mCurrentACacheBlock; // if any

   and does the shipping of
   curacb, when it is deemed
   ready, with

        /** we are the only one that can falsify any of these conditions,
            if they are currently true, so we don't have to lock until we
            know we want to do so. right??
         */

        bool keytest =
          curacb != 0 &&          // have a car and
          !crbi.isEmpty() &&      // more empty cars are available and
          !crbo.isFull() &&       // more full cars are shippable and
          acl1.readyToClose();
  ..      if (keytest) {  // current car is ready to go
          bool got;
  ..      { // SHIPPING
            ACacheBlock & olb = *curacb;
            ACacheBlockPayload & opay = olb.payload();
  ..        olb.closeTC(opay.getCurrentPayloadSize()); // close the car
  ..        got = crbo.add(acl1.mCurrentCarIndex); // hand control back to comm
  ..        HBASSERT_EQ(got,true);
          }
          // RECEIVING
          acl1.setupNewCar(crbi);
        } else if (curacb == 0) { // if have no car
          if (!crbi.isEmpty()) { // ready to init?
            acl1.setupNewCar(crbi) ;
          } else HBNOTE(MT-CRBI);
        } else {
          // else not ready for anything
        }

so it 'ships' by pushing curacb
back to HN via crbo, and
'receives' by pulling another
index - which might not exist! -
off of crbi.

So in this privatecontrol
scheme, a key point is that
shipping and receiving can be
aynchronous to each other, and
'everybody' is prepared to deal
with curacb == NULL.

So perhaps we could just keep
stealing all this? Need to see
who calls updateCars, and so
forth.. but we imagine HB would
call an analogous update cars
for IH?

[371:

Fri Sep 18 10:58:31 2026 OK, I
think stuff like

    {152:37 35988 0 2,3 hn EPuo:[IHEPi:IHUB:2] c5 h0o255 g5o0 S0 R0 fs3}

and

means that updateOps is getting
called too.[372:

Fri Sep 18 14:56:05 2026 And
those S0 R0 numbers means
nothing is moving ATM, which
certainly fits.

And the (generic) action of
updateOps is to

1. handle a complete arrived car
   by calling down to recvTC and

2. if recvTC returns true, mark
   there's another car here and
   one less car gone, set up
   to return true later, and go
   to 1.

3. and when recvTC returns
   false, stop the receive loop
   and then

4. handle a closed complete car
   by advancing it to the
   departing state, and[374: and
   note that magic happens
   INSIDE getClosedTCPtrIfAny(),
   which is where the COMP2COMM
   crbo gets checked/popped.
   :374]

5. if the car isn't (now) in the
   departing state, stop the
   send loop, otherwise

6. call down to shipTC to
   actually xmit the car (which
   in the current code base is
   always handled by
   T6EP<>::shipTC, which handles
   shipping both to the host and
   to T6 destinations.)

7. When the send loop stops,
   return true if any cars were
   received or sent, or false
   otherwise.

[375:

Fri Sep 18 16:30:35 2026 So,
when IH processing wants to ship
a car (in any direction,
including routing), it should

(1) have a car on the
    appropriate platorm,
(2) mark it TCState::CLOSED, and
(3) push its index onto the
    appropriate COMP2COMM

and if it can get past (1),
there, the rest of that can
happen 'anytime' as long as it's
HB that's doing it.

 - try to deal with its current
   car. if it's occupied,
   dispatch on it.

HN sends empty cars if it's got

makes four, and the odd man
extra just circulates and keeps

[379:

Fri Sep 18 23:45:35 2026 And the
users of IH see what, and work
how?

Well the official use case is
shipping caches back to follower
regions at the end of a leader's
reign. And I guess hub/HB is in
charge of that, and it will have
some little state machine
running it, which will grab an
empty IH car, fill it with sites
destined for whatever neighbor
the state machine is working on,
and push it back to HN for
transit.

And it won't matter if those
cars arrive out of order as long
as they all arrive, because they
will be self-documenting and
independent of each other.

And those cars will end up
getting passed to the remote
hub/HB, which will update the
grid and then .. what? Return
'the car' to the sender? But on
a bank shot it's not even the
same car. Just mark it empty and
treat it as any other newly
emptied IH in HB land?

If we're not going to try to
interlock the whole cache
flushing process; if we're just
going to have a 'generous
timeout urgh', then there's [no
reason] to return it.

So the leader sends such packets
to all its ngbs until everything
has been shipped, and then it
responds to the cache-flush
phaser bolt? So the racy part is
just the followers getting the
last packets and applying them
before another phaser bolt goes
out.

If we wanted to get fancy..
which we don't.

..but we could have the
followers defer responding to
the cache-flush bolt until
they'd applied all the sites
they were expecting to get.

And all this also happens in
reverse when the leader is
loading its caches when starting
its reign.

 - say we're not ready to do
   events, by returning false
   from getRandomEvtCtr?

 - wait for ngbs to send us
   their sites? how do we know
   when they're done, and indeed
   how many ngbs we should be
   expecting sites from?

 - so (miracle happens) then
   we're ready to LEAD.

[391:

Sun Sep 20 10:01:13 2026 How to
beginMode:FOLLOW

 - say we're not ready to do
   events.

 - have previously been told WHO
   WE'RE FOLLOWING

 - send (in multiple packets
   typically), our atoms that
   are visible to the LEADER

 - then we are ready to FOLLOW

But here's a thing: On a 2x2
cell, we will typically be
following multiple ngbs at once.

But I'd really rather not deal
with each ngb individually in
that regard. So I'd really
rather having FOLLOWING mode
mean pull in to limited sites
range on all four sides whether
or not there's a leader out
beyond that side?

Well, I guess the phaser bolt
theory of it is that everybody
knows who (in the cell) is the
leader and therefore they 'can'
know which sides they need to
pull in on. The aggravating
thing is that in 'the general
case' - meaning no edges -
followers DO need to pull in on
all sides. And it's only because
there ARE edges that all the
special cases pop up.

(And just for the record we do
note there are at least two
kinds of edges: HARD EDGES at
the ends of the universe versus
CARD EDGES where there's another
card beyond in that direction
that we eventually need to
reach.)

Mon Sep 21 12:07:36 2026 OK,
that's coming along, but we had
another KISS idea last night:

 - Screw 'general interhub
   packet routing'. All we need
   at present is the Moore ngb,
   and the only hard part is the
   antidiagonal, to the NE and
   the SW.

 - So let's waste some (moore,
   hehe) space and do this:

   = Have the InterHub block be
     eight-ways instead of four,

   = and ALSO have a separate
     set of four 'corner'
     blocks, whose only purpose
     is to forward cars
     clockwise around that
     particular corner.

 - So when a hub wants to send a
   IH car NE, say, it sends it
   to the SE corner block of the
   hub to its N,

 - And when a hub discovers
   there's a complete packet in
   its SE corner block, it
   automatically sends it to the
   SW entry of the eight-way IH
   EP, and then spoils that
   packet or something.

 - Then when that NE hub wants
   to send 'that same IH car'
   back to its SW, it sends it
   to the corresponding position
   in the SW corner of the hub
   to its S,

 - which forwards it clockwise
   to the NE 8way.

  typedef TCStorage<InterHubBlock,3> InterHubStorage;

  XX(CORNERHUB,CHUB,?????,InterHubStorage,4u)            \

price out..

(/ 20800 5) 4160 bytes per IH car

(+ (* 8 3) (* 4 3)) 36 IH cars

(* 4160 36) 149760B ~150KB total

vs (* 20800 4) 83200B ~80KB
before.

requirement. Or more if we
decide three cars per dir8
aren't enough.

Mon Sep 21 16:08:43 2026 So,
let's try some little just-so
stories about what we want.

One day, Bub the Hub was named
the leader of the local region.
Bub was so excited!

And all of Bub's friends were
excited for Bub too! And they
wanted to contribute their
knowledge to help with Bub's
success!

For example, Bub's friend WTub
wanted to send Bub copies of all
the atoms in its 'East Sites
Range' (138,10..148,118]. And
wow that's a lot of atoms!
That's.. let's see, that's

(148-138)*(118-10), which is
    10   *   108, which is

one thousand and eighty atoms,
to be exact.

Now, WTub couldn't send that
many atoms all at once. So WTub
divided them up into four groups
of 270, intending to send each
group separately, one after the
other!

But WTub only had three cars
that went to Bub's place. But
WTub knew that Bub would send
the cars back as soon as WTub's
atoms had been copied out of it.

So WTub started filling cars and
sending them east to Bub's
place. And when Bub received
each one, it copied the atoms
out.

And where did Bub put those
atoms? They came from WTub, so
did Bub put them in its West
Sites Range? Oh no no no! Bub
already had its Own Atoms in its
West Sites Range. They said: Go
put those atoms somewhere else,
Bub!

And so Bub put the atoms in its
West Cache Range: (0,10..10,118]
instead. And look at that:
(10-0) * (118-10) is ALSO one
thousand and eighty - exactly
the same size and shape as
WTub's East Sites Range! So each
atom in WTub's East Sites Range
has a matching spot in Bub's
West Cache Range!

And once the car was all copied,
Bub marked it empty and sent it
back to WTub, so WTub could fill
it up again!

And after a little while, WTub
finished sending all those atoms
to Bub. You're all set by me!
WTub's last car said to Bub.

Now, around that same time,
another one of Bub's friends
from the neighborhood, named
NEub, also wanted to send Bub
some atoms, from its 'South West
Range' (10,108..20,118]. That
was only one hundred atoms to
send, so it could fit them all
in one car.

But oh no! NEub couldn't send
that car directly to Bub, like
WTub could. Why not? People
said: That's Just How The World
Works, which is really no answer
at all, but the fact remained.

So NEub relied on a little help
from its friend, who NEub called
'STub', but Bub called 'ETub'.
Isn't that confusing! Everybody
has their own names for each of
their neighbors! What a funny
neighborhood!

Let's stick with Bub's names so
we don't get too confused. Since
NEub couldn't talk directly to
Bub, it sent its car to ETub's
NorthWest Corner Block! But what
is THAT?

Is the NorthWest Corner Block
the same as the NorthWest Cache
Range? No no no, no!

Is the NorthWest Corner Block
the same as the NorthWest Sites
Range? No no no, no!

The NorthWest Corner Block is
its Own Separate Thing! And its
whole job is to help its friend
to its north communicate with
its friend to the west. It's
such a good pal!

So - again sticking to Bub's
names for everybody - NEub sent
a car with one hundred atoms to
the NorthWest Corner Block of
ETub.

And then ETub sent that car on
to Bub, and Bub unloaded the car
into its NorthEast Cache Range.
[414:

Mon Sep 21 20:57:29 2026 Well
there's a lot more that could be
told in that fairy tale but I'm
pretty much out of gas for it.

I want to pass closer to code.

Q+A:

Q: How does Bub/HB send a packet
   NE?

A: It draws an empty IH-NE car
   from the NE crbi of the 8way,
   fills it with stuff, and then
   marks it closed.

Q: Then what happens?

A: HN calls shipTC which
   recognizes its a diagonal
   shipment, so it computes

     dest8 = clockwiseDir8(D8_NE)

   and gets D8_NT, then it
   computes

     dest4 = dir8ToDir4CW(D8_NT)

   which returns D4_N.

Q: Then what happens?

A: Then shipTC computes
     oppD4 = oppositeDir4(D4_N)
   and gets
     D4_S,
   and then computes
     oppD8 = dir4ToDir8(oppD4)
   and gets
     D8_ST,
   and then computes
     corner8 = clockwiseDir8(D8_ST)
   and gets
     D8_SE

Q: So what? What then?

A: The shipTC finds the address
   of the SouthEast Corner
   block, which is responsible
   for sending packets FROM the
   South TO the East, so the
   packet takes 90 degree CW
   turn.

   Then shipTC ships the car to
   the matching spot of the
   SouthEast Corner block of the
   hub in the dest8 (==D8_NT)
   direction, and

   then marks the car shipped
   locally.

Q: Then what happens?

A: Eventually, the HN to Bub's
   north detects a complete
   packet in its SE Corner
   block, and it wants to sends
   it on to its east ngb, which
   is Bub's NE ngb.

Q: And where exactly does it
   send it?

A: The SouthEast corner block
   always sends packets to the
   east, and places them in the
   corresponding slot of its
   east ngb's SouthWest InterHub
   on the 8way, and then marks
   the packet gone locally.

And that's how Bub sends a
packet to its NE ngb's SW
InterHub, without going all the
way around the universe.

And when NEub wants to return
the car to Bub, it will end up
sending it south to the NW
Corner Block, which will give a
90 degree CW turn (in from ST ->
out to WT) and thus send it back
to Bub, where it lands in Bub's
NE Interhub right back where it
started.

Wed Sep 30 01:56:44 2026 So we
just saw a race instance. This
one, I pretty strongly believe,
went like this:

    {74:178 0 0 4,7 hn FBCNus:(4,7)}
4,7 -> I am a HUB
 .. {74:179 0 0 4,7 hn FBCNthem:(3,6)}
3,6 -> They are an EWP
 .. {74:182 0 0 4,7 hn fBCin0:IC_EWP}
their image code              ^^^^^^
    {74:184 0 0 4,7 hn =(4,7)}
ournoc0
    {74:185 0 0 4,7 hn =(3,6)}
theirnoc0
    {74:186 0 0 4,7 hn ibh.mEntries:4}
their ibh has 4 entires
    {74:207 0 0 4,7 hn bc:6}
one of which is 6 == EWPCARS
    {184:51 0 0 4,7 hn PREFAIL:EwpBlock}
but I - a HUB - am trying to
init MY EwpBlocks..
    {184:52 0 0 4,7 hn PREFAILsize:1024}
..each of which is 1KB..
    {184:53 0 0 4,7 hn PREFAILnext:4}
..and I want to mark it OUTBOUND_DEPARTED..
    {184:55 0 0 4,7 hn HBANG!:IC_HUB}
..AND IT BETTER BE CLOSED BEFORE I DO SO..
    {184:55 0 0 4,7 hn =3}
..but it's 3==INBOUND_DEPARTED, not..
    {184:55 0 0 4,7 hn =2}
..2==CLOSED.

So I - a HUB, a proud, sensible
HUB - chose to honorably go
bang.
[492:

Wed Sep 30 02:12:00 2026 And the
explanation, I believe, is that
3,6 finished its initialization
earlier than 4,7, and started
shipping ewp cars to 4,7. Such
cars would correctly be marked
INBOUND_DEPARTED, because a HUB
is always inbound of an EWP.

Wed Sep 30 02:47:50 2026 OK, so
we looked through EWP LiveB and
noticed a substantial kind of
lack of waiting for stuff.. so
we made a hack:

  void stepB(HostBlock & hb) {
    if (theT6BoltResponder.shallWeCarryOn())
      processEwpCars(hb,false);
  }

so we won't sent any EWP cars
unless the bolt responder says
(1) the current bolt is CARRY_ON
(2) and it's been returned to host

and (with a massive N of 1,) we
have not seen 'the' race recur.

(0) init: HOLD_AT_BIRTH

(6) SUSPEND_EVENTS
(7) SAVE LEADER CACHES
(8) goto (3)

        mInterHubHackThreadPtr = std::make_unique<std::thread>([this]() {
 ..       for (MFM::u64 i = 0u; ++i != 0u; ) {

[520:

Wed Sep 30 22:22:39 2026
Tearing. So, 'the' problem with

      (0) NEW LEADER: X
      (1) LOAD CACHE
      (2) CARRY_ON
      (3) SUSPEND EVENTS
      (4) SAVE CACHE

as the supercycle is that - if X
is an arg - it saves the cache
at the end even if caller wanted
to call again with X.

But let's fold that into the
super cycle. We'll pick the X,
and we'll short-circuit
internally if we repick X?

But then we'd need to remember
the 'final' non-X pick, for next
time, or else we might
'inefficiently' reload the
caches we just saved.

We could have caller shuffle the
leaders, pass it as an arg, and
then save caches blindly.

Or we could have caller repick
until the leader changes, and
call with that. So the next
leader will be different than
the current, but there's no
assurance of uniformity.
Combined with a significantly
variable amount of time to
CARRY_ON, I'd think that
wouldn't be too biased..

as suggested by the XXX. And
that's called exclusively by:

      bool beatTheStandardHeartbeat(HostBlock & hb) {
        // HEARTBEAT AND BOLT RESPONSE
        u32 oldspin = fAll.mHeartSpin++;
        if ((oldspin & 0x3f) == 0) {
          theT6BoltResponder.boltResponderAllHarts();
          if (oldspin == 0) {
            hb.hartbeat(fAll.mHartNum);
            return true;
          }
        }
        return false;
      }

at the moment. Which is nice in
that it's "automatic" and "out
of everybody's hands".. but for
unique responses to particular
bolts, we need to put it into
some people's hands.

I want to think about a table of
hart x bolt, saying which harts
want a custom reply to which
bolts. (And yes I know that's
not so MINIMAL sounding (and I
also know it's sounding like
fucking TaskManager all over
again, which is a hill we do not
want to die on again.))

then what?

    bool T6BoltResponder::isReadyToAcknowledge() ;

from all harts, and let.. urgh.

  bool __attribute__((weak)) readyToAcknowledgeBoltB() { return true; }
  bool __attribute__((weak)) readyToAcknowledgeBoltT0() { return true; }

  bool __attribute__((weak)) readyToAcknowledgeBoltNC() { return true; }

and let the harts-per-image deal
with it however they want to.
That's MINIMALMINIMAL in that
it's per-hart only, with no
concept of 'tasks' (ahrm) of
which a single hart might well
have multiple to deal with.

Could we eek out just a tiny bit
of flexibility with like levels:

  bool readyToAcknowledgeBoltB() {
    if (!imageReadyToAckBoltB()) return false;
    if (!crosslibReadyToAckBoltB()) return false;
    return true;
  }

and we have no weak hackery, so
each image has to define

 bool imageReadyToAckBoltB() {

[541:

Thu Oct  1 11:36:47 2026 Looks
like it's in column-order? --

        static UxC makeSuperCellCoordFromLeaderCode(u32 leadercode) {
          UxC ret;
          ret.x = (leadercode>>1)&1;
          ret.y = (leadercode>>0)&1;
          return ret;
        }

        static u8 makeLeaderCodeFromSuperCellCoord(UxC sc) {
          if (sc.x > 1 || sc.y > 1) return U8_MAX;
          u8 ret = 0;
          if (sc.x == 1) ret |= 1<<1;
          if (sc.y == 1) ret |= 1<<0;
          return ret;
        }

but yeah, it's 0..3, or 255 for
none.

[591:

Fri Oct  2 13:19:26 2026 So
okay: What would it mean for a
follower to have a plan for what
needs to be sent to leader?
TODO:

 - Follower needs to know which
   Dir8 points to the leader.
   (NOTE THESE Dir8s WILL OFTEN
   POINT TO A DIFFERENT
   SUPERCELL.)

 - Follower needs to know if
   there actually is a leader in
   that Dir8, given local
   circumstances.

 - Follower needs to know (say
   the) S8C offsets from its own
   sites to the leader's cache.

 - Follower needs to know the
   size of the rectangle to be
   moved.

 - Follower needs to know where
   it is in the process of
   sending those sites.

 - Follower needs to know what
   to do until all sites have
   been sent.

 - Follower finally must know
   when it's done and then ack
   the LOAD_CACHE bolt.

    to LOAD_CACHE completely in

    acknowledging the LOAD_CACHE
    bolt.

the outer class has any data

[618:

Sat Oct  3 10:59:18 2026 I think
I want to try being a bit more
systematic about coord units and
naming. Dealing with like three
spatial scales in one code
stretch is a lot. 

SPATIAL UNIT NAMES

  UCT6 ('compact T6' - smallest units)
   internal: -none-
   position: CT6C (coord)
   distance: CT6V (vector)
   external: UREG
   superpower: physical computation

  UREG (region - bigger units)
   internal: UCT6
   position: REGC
   distance: REGV
   external: USCL
   superpower: defines simulation space

  USCL (supercell - bigger units)
   internal: UREG
   position: SCLC
   distance: SCLV
   external: UBLK
   superpower: guides data movement

  UBLK (blackhole chips - bigger units)
   internal: USCL
   position: BLKC
   distance: BLKV
   external: UQBX
   superpower: fucking fast data movement

  UQBX (quietboxes - biggest units ATM)
   internal: UBLK
   position: QBXC (only (0,0) atm)
   distance: QBXV
   external: -none-
   superpower: one is owned by me

[619:

Sat Oct  3 11:40:14 2026 Well
that's kind of clean in some
ways. Also problematic because
we'll (I think) want to have
USCL span cards down the road..
So we'll see.

But it seems plausible-ish and
cleaner-ish for now.

Sat Oct  3 17:40:01 2026 OK
after a bit more tweaking it
looks like we are generating
plausible, existing NoC0 coords
for the up to eight Dir8 hub
ngbs. Now we need to know the
REGV of USCL of each of them, to
determine which of them are
currently leaders.

So how do we map to USCL and
REGV inside SCL? Our main defs
so far are:

        U8C uct6_per_ureg(2,2);        // UCT6 conversion to UREG
        U8C ureg_per_uscl(2,2);        // UREG conversion to USCL

        U8C ourCT6C = U8C::makeCT6CoordFromTLBI(hb.mTLBI);
        U8C ourREGC = ourCT6C / uct6_per_ureg; //< our position in region space

        U8C ourCT6V_of_REGC = ourCT6C % uct6_per_ureg;           //< our UCT6 offset in region ourREGC
        U8C ourREGC_in_UCT6 = ourREGC * uct6_per_ureg;           //< UL corner of our region in CT6
        U8C ourSCLC_in_REGC = ourREGC / ureg_per_uscl;           //< UL corner of our supercell in region coords
        U8C ourREGV_of_SCLC = ourSCLC_in_REGC % ureg_per_uscl;   //< our UREG offset in supercell ourSCLC

and then, given a d8, like:

          S8C d8ngbREGV = S8C::makeS8CFromDir8(d8);

          /*  /-CT6V-\  = /-REGV-\  *  /--UCT6/UREG---\  */
          S8C d8ngbCT6V = d8ngbREGV * S8C(uct6_per_ureg);

          /*  /-CT6C-\  =  /-CT6C---\  +  /-CT6V-\  */
          S8C d8ngbCT6C = S8C(ourCT6C) + d8ngbCT6V;

          U8C ud8ngbCT6C;
          if (!d8ngbCT6C.toU8C(ud8ngbCT6C)) continue; // off chip N or W

          U8C d8ngbNoC0C = U8C::makeNoC0CoordFromCT6Coord(ud8ngbCT6C);
          if (d8ngbNoC0C == U8C::UxC_MAX_VAL) continue; // off chip E or S

so.. if we had d8ngbREGC, we
could do like

          U8C d8nSCLC_in_REGC = d8nREGC / ureg_per_uscl;   //< UL corner of d8n supercell in region coords
          U8C d8nREGV_of_SCLC = d8nREGC % ureg_per_uscl;   //< d8n UREG offset in supercell ourSCLC

and then if we had ldrREGV_of_SCLC (?)
then we could do like

  if (d8nREGV_of_SCLC != ldrREGV_of_SCLC) continue;

and we would then be in The
Correct Place to define a
CacheLoader.

That seems really quite
plausible. But it's getting to
be dinner time!

Sat Oct  3 23:21:16 2026 So the
main thing we'll want is this
stuff that relates Dir8s and
site ranges and cache ranges:

        using Dir8Ranges = U8CRange[D8_NE+1];

        static constexpr const Dir8Ranges CACHE_RANGES = {
          //  (start,          end]
          { { OW, 0 },        {TW-OW, OH} }, // NT
          { { 0, 0 },         {OW, OH} },    // NW
          { { 0, OH },        {OW, TH-OH} }, // WT
          { { 0, TH-OH },     {OW, TH} },    // SW
          { { OW, TH-OH },    {TW-OW, TH} }, // ST
          { { TW-OW, TH-OH }, {TW, TH} },    // SE
          { { TW-OW, OH },    {TW, TH-OH} }, // ET
          { { TW-OW, 0 },     {TW, OH} },    // NE
        };

ground truth for the Dir8 cache
blocks defined in terms of
'B'ase W+H, 'O'verlap W+H, and
'T'otal W+H.

        static constexpr const Dir8Ranges SITE_RANGES = {
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_NT],{ 0, OH }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_NW],{ OW, OH }),
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_WT],{ OW, 0 }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_SW],{ OW,(u8)-OH }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_ST],{ 0,(u8)-OH }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_SE],{(u8)-OW,(u8)-OH }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_ET],{(u8)-OW, 0 }), 
          U8CRange(CACHE_RANGES[D8_NE],{(u8)-OW, OH }), 
        };

and ditto for the Dir8 site
blocks, defined as mods on the
cache blocks using the weird
'offset' ctor:

        constexpr UxCRange(const UxCRange base, const UC offset)
          : start(base.start + offset)
          , stop(base.stop + offset)
        { }

and finally the rest of the
tiles sites, part of no cache: 

        static constexpr const U8CRange HIDDEN_RANGE = { { 2*OW,2*OH }, { TW-2*OW, TH-2*OH } };

   be for local cache mgmt, then
   we could say that in states

     PhaserBolt::CMD_NEW_LEADER

   in the followers, while in

     PhaserBolt::CMD_SAVE_CACHE

   we want to push empties
   toward the leader.

Sun Oct  4 23:54:21 2026 Well,
it kind of doesn't, in the sense
of:

      bool T6BoltResponder::isBoltAnyOfThese(PhaserBolt::CmdMask mask) {
        // RACY RACY NO LOCK
        if (mLastSeqnoReturned != mLastSeqnoArrived) return false;
 ..

so a pre-consensus bolt always
replies 'no' to questions about
whether it's type this or that.

So that means pre-consensus
SAVE_CACHE will NOT match given
like: 

   theT6BoltResponder.continueWhenBoltIsAnyOf(PhaserBolt::CmdMask::SAVE_CACHE)

but it WILL match given like:

   theT6BoltResponder.continueWhenBoltIsNoneOf(PhaserBolt::CmdMask::SAVE_CACHE)

because once again we're
returning a fucking bool instead
of a ternary of some kind.

Is anybody using
continueWhenBoltIsNoneOf?
